* DONE [#B] Fix Eternal Terminal 7.0.0 Linux Home Manager build
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-01a
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-12 Wed 10:16]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-12 Wed 13:59]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-12 Wed 13:59]
:CLOSE_REASON: Fixed the consumer-owned Eternal Terminal 7.0 header drift and proved the full Andoria Home Manager closure.
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

The user reported that Andoria-08 Home Manager failed at eternal-terminal-7.0.0 patchPhase because the consumer-owned override targeted nonexistent src/terminal/PsuedoUserTerminal.hpp. Diagnose the upstream/source drift, fix the owning Nix seam declaratively, add Linux realization coverage, and make the shared-work update workflow run consumer checks before its first switch attempt. Main CI is a separate failure tracked by nix-g5k.

** Acceptance Criteria

The Eternal Terminal override no longer targets a nonexistent source path; its package builds for the shared-work Linux platform; the relevant Home Manager configuration builds without activation; a regression check detects future source-path drift; repository-wide CI status is reported separately and not inferred from focused local gates.

* DONE [#C] Make the signed-tip publication contract executable
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-0aj9
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-13 Thu 16:46]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-13 Thu 17:50]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-13 Thu 17:50]
:CLOSE_REASON: Completed in the frozen Gitea publication transaction: literal/revalidated authority, isolated fresh ref snapshot, exact signed tip and raw-history range, conflicting dry-run/publish rejection, supported transactional recovery only, replacement/graft/shallow defenses, and signal-safe post-push readback state. Strict 52-test suite and Bash/shfmt/ShellCheck/Ruff/diff gates pass; independent exact-hash signal rereview CLEAR at bin/publish 294583bd and publish-slow-test.py 9b26d34e.
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

Documentation promises that Gitea contains the signed revision / a signed branch tip, but bin/publish exits successfully when already current before verifying the target tip, and may exclude commits reachable from another origin branch. Tests accept unsigned no-op and new-branch tips. Acceptance: either verify the exact target tip and every newly published commit on every path, including no-op and new branch, or explicitly narrow all user-facing contracts with matching tests; unsigned target tips must not be reported as signed publication.

* DONE [#B] Restore sandbox hook cost attribution through the host authority path
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-0b4z
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-13 Thu 15:47]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-13 Thu 18:28]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-13 Thu 18:28]
:CLOSE_REASON: Sandbox cost attribution now reaches a profile-bound host-validated and durable idempotent SQLite boundary with real end-to-end regression coverage.
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

Baseline hook handling recorded cost events before sandbox branching; current authority rewrite records them only in the non-sandbox branch, and the sandbox bridge carries no cost payload. Restore exactly-once profile-bound cost attribution for sandbox hooks through the host-validated path, with same-attempt retry and real sandbox regressions.

** Notes

Current-byte independent re-audit found the sandbox cost producer path, but exactly-once durability is not yet satisfied: internal/costs/watcher.go deletes an artifact immediately after channel enqueue, while cmd/agent-deck/main.go persists later and discards WriteCostEvent errors, so a transient SQLite failure loses the only copy. Start also performs no preexisting-file scan, leaving events created while no profile TUI is watching stranded. Keep nix-0b4z open; require ack-after-successful-persistence/idempotent retry plus startup rescan regressions.

* DONE [#C] Install pi-mulligan as a managed Pi extension                  :context:extension:pi:
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-0pr
:LABELS:   ["context","extension","pi","pi-gallery"]
:ISSUE_TYPE: feature
:CREATED:  [2026-08-12 Wed 20:51]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-13 Thu 22:09]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-13 Thu 21:59]
:CLOSE_REASON: Dropped as speculative upstream product scope: no maintainer-approved architecture or owner-fork authority. Recreate one concrete task only after an authoritative bounded-history API exists.
:EXTERNAL_REF: https://github.com/dabstractor/pi-mulligan
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

Integrate https://github.com/dabstractor/pi-mulligan into this repository using the same immutable source, package, gallery, renderer, manifest, and behavioral-test pattern as the other managed Pi extensions. Upstream describes a zero-configuration Pi extension that provides agent-callable rewind, shrink, cancel, and audit tools plus human checkpoint/audit commands; it currently declares compatibility with Pi 0.84.x and is loaded from src/index.ts or as a Pi package.

Do not use pi install against mutable user state or copy files into ~/.pi. Establish exact managed-Pi API compatibility first, preserve Nix ownership of generated leaves and Pi ownership of mutable session/settings state, and account for the extension's persisted context markers in the bounded-history certification surface.

** Acceptance Criteria

Pin an immutable reviewed upstream revision and package it through the existing Pi gallery/managed-extension abstractions; render it only for the intended Pi profiles without editing mutable Pi state. Prove compatibility with the exact managed Pi version (upstream currently declares Pi 0.84.x) and fail closed rather than silently loading an incompatible API. A behavioral managed-Pi test must load and reload the extension with no mulligan settings, observe its four agent tools and three human commands, exercise a representative context transform without losing the on-disk audit trail, and show existing extension order, metadata, routing, state ownership, and selection remain intact. Add source/update metadata and documentation following the existing extension pattern; run the focused gallery checks, all-systems evaluation, and relevant package/system builds. Activation remains separately authorized.

* DONE [#B] Reject case-insensitive profile-root aliases during migration
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-0sae
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-13 Thu 16:21]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-13 Thu 18:31]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-13 Thu 18:31]
:CLOSE_REASON: Profile migration now rejects only actual filesystem aliases before effects while preserving distinct case-sensitive profile roots; independent audit is clear.
:SOURCE_REPO: agent-deck
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/Products/agent-deck-runtime-bindings-cleanup
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

Agent Deck profile migration currently compares source and target names bytewise, so work to Work passes the same-profile guard but resolves to the same DB and sidecar roots on default case-insensitive macOS filesystems; migration can then self-unlink authority state. Acceptance: storage/profile identity detects path aliases using the actual profile-root filesystem semantics before any write, refuses the migration without side effects, and regressions cover case-only and separator/dot aliases without conflating distinct case-sensitive roots.

:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0

Agent Deck StateDB writable and read-only openers still pass profile state.db paths directly to SQLite and do not provide race-free O_NOFOLLOW ancestry/leaf protection. A trusted path can be replaced by a symlink between validation and open, exposing or mutating state outside the profile root. Acceptance: profile database, WAL, and SHM admission rejects symlinked or substituted ancestors/leaves at the authoritative open boundary; safe same-owner regular paths continue to work; mutation-killing race/hostile-path tests cover writable and read-only/context openers without inventing a second database authority.

* DEFER [#C] Investigate intermittent zsh first-prompt history stalls
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-2hjj
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-15 Sat 15:02]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-15 Sat 15:02]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:DEFERRED: [2026-09-14 Mon 15:02]
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

Opening a new interactive zsh on Hera is intermittently delayed before the
first prompt. The strongest user reproduction was a new tmux window opened in
`~/src/nix` on 2026-08-15 at about 14:59 PDT: the prompt took more than 15
seconds. This is distinct from the previously fixed compaudit/zcompdump path.

Evidence gathered without changing shell behavior:

- Current Darwin generation 1093 was reactivated at 07:48, but it points to an
  already-built system generation. Its generated `.config/zsh/.zshrc` is
  byte-identical to generation 1092. The current repository candidate does not
  modify `config/zsh.nix`, `config/johnw.nix`, `config/home.nix`, or
  `config/darwin.nix`.
- Controlled interactive-shell runs are bimodal. Immediate repeats often take
  0.10-0.14 seconds, while observed slow runs took 3.41, 5.49, 6.63, 7.32, and
  9.65 seconds. The user independently observed a real tmux first-prompt delay
  over 15 seconds.
- A field-filtered child-name trace of a 6.63-second slow run saw no Nix or
  direnv child. After the ordinary zsh/carapace initialization, no external
  child was present for the multi-second tail. Direct `direnv export zsh` took
  0.09 seconds and the repository `.envrc` only applies `.envrc.cache`; it does
  not contain `use flake` or invoke Nix.
- Standalone initializers were fast: Starship about 0.06 seconds cold, fzf about
  0.04, carapace about 0.05, and zoxide/direnv-hook effectively zero. A zprof
  source of `.zshrc` accounted for about 1.19 seconds total, with no component
  explaining the intermittent multi-second wait. `compinit -C` itself was
  about 0.21 ms, so the activation-time zcompdump fix is not implicated.
- The managed history file was 4,710,652 bytes and 126,540 lines. At the end of
  the user's delayed tmux startup its mtime was 14:59:13, immediately before
  the prompt appeared. Hera has many concurrent long-lived zsh processes.
- `config/zsh.nix` configures a 500,000-entry saved history with sharing and
  appending enabled. The generated `.zshrc` enables `SHARE_HISTORY`,
  `APPEND_HISTORY`, and `HIST_FCNTL_LOCK`. Zsh documents that shared history
  imports new commands and appends typed commands, while `HIST_FCNTL_LOCK`
  serializes history writes with `fcntl`.

Current diagnosis: intermittent history-file lock/import/merge coordination is
the best-supported seam. It explains low CPU, no external child, the bimodal
latency, concurrent shells, and the history-file update at prompt time. This is
not yet filesystem-confirmed because no lock-owner snapshot was captured during
the exact 15-second stall. Do not attribute the delay to Nix or direnv without
new evidence.

No history behavior change is authorized. In particular, do not disable
`SHARE_HISTORY`, change persistence timing, reduce retained history, or activate
a shell configuration merely to make the benchmark green. A possible future
tradeoff, only after owner approval, is to replace live cross-shell importing
with `INC_APPEND_HISTORY_TIME`: commands would still persist after completion,
but already-open shells would not immediately import commands entered elsewhere.

Future investigation should measure actual tmux time-to-first-prompt rather
than conflate startup with `zsh -lic exit` history flushing; capture narrowly
filtered child/process and history-lock evidence during a slow sample; and
compare unchanged behavior with any explicitly approved alternative.

** Acceptance Criteria

Capture a slow tmux time-to-first-prompt sample and identify or falsify history locking with direct evidence; preserve current history semantics unless the owner explicitly approves a tradeoff; verify repeated real first prompts without Nix or direnv children before closing.

Mapping: 9d1874ee→d8041eda, 15540784→46120df2, 50d02ed5→abb10aed, d707baf0→61db9788, 82beaff3→41f5888d, 740afdc9→8666a51a. For every pair, tree ID, stable patch ID, raw message, author/committer identity and timestamp, changed-file list, and position match; range-diff is six exact equals; the final tree is unchanged. Objects were staged under a temporary VPS ref, main moved with an old-tip compare-and-swap, and publication used an exact lease on remote base 5c2256f5. Because the old six commits were unpublished, the accepted update was a fast-forward. Gitea main, origin/main, and VPS main now equal 8666a51a; the temporary ref is deleted and the checkout is clean. No lock update, build, activation, or unrelated cleanup was performed.
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

The VPS /etc/nixos checkout is clean and currently contains a six-commit linear unpublished chain above origin/main: four unsigned commits (9d1874ee, 15540784, 50d02ed5, and d707baf0) followed by two GOOD-signed commits (82beaff3 and 740afdc9). The owner authorized rewriting and signing only the first two unsigned commits. Rewriting either ancestor necessarily regenerates all later commit IDs, including both signed descendants, so no history mutation may proceed until the owner explicitly authorizes the complete six-commit result. Preserve the checkout and do not fetch, publish, update locks, build, or activate as part of history repair without the separately recorded authorities.

** Acceptance Criteria

With explicit owner authorization covering the complete six-commit chain, preserve every commit tree, patch content, author, message, file list, and order while validly signing the four unsigned changes and regenerating the two signed descendants. Verify signatures, tree IDs, stable patch IDs, range-diff, authors, messages, and file lists. Publication or force-with-lease, paired nix-config lock updates, /etc/nixos/./build build and switch, runtime acceptance, rollback verification, and checkout cleanup remain separately gated actions.

:LABELS:   []

:CREATED_BY: johnw

:CLOSE_REASON: Completed in the frozen Gitea publication transaction: literal/revalidated authority, isolated fresh ref snapshot, exact signed tip and raw-history range, conflicting dry-run/publish rejection, supported transactional recovery only, replacement/graft/shallow defenses, and signal-safe post-push readback state. Strict 52-test suite and Bash/shfmt/ShellCheck/Ruff/diff gates pass; independent exact-hash signal rereview CLEAR at bin/publish 294583bd and publish-slow-test.py 9b26d34e.
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

bin/publish validates raw/effective origin URLs, then runs a gate and later fetches/pushes/readbacks by mutable remote name. A same-user process or gate can change .git/config or URL rewrites after validation, redirect both push and readback, and produce false success. Acceptance: bind publication to immutable validated destinations or revalidate raw/effective fetch and push URLs after the gate and immediately around every network mutation/readback; tests mutate the remote during the gate and prove no non-Gitea network action.

Independent fess audit of commit 5016353 found that bin/publish inherits graph selectors and Git templates, checks signatures through checkout-local Git configuration, and does not bind the real push to the fetched target tip. The consumer gate also accepts computed URL expressions that can hide a retired GitHub nix-config source. Fix the isolated transport and literal-source policy with focused mutation tests; preserve the single Gitea authority and fast-forward-only transaction.

* DEFER [#B] Port managed Pi Goal X bounded history to 0.27.4
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-58dc
:LABELS:   []

:CREATED:  [2026-08-17 Mon 19:24]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-17 Mon 19:24]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

Pi Goal X 0.27.4 cannot be adopted by the managed gallery yet. The strict 0.19 bounded-history patch no longer applies. An isolated 0.27 port proved the basic streaming and accounting fixtures, but independent review found its upstream all-goal checkpoint/cache retains O(distinct historical goal IDs), making append checkpointing O(goal cardinality), and found stale-checkpoint event loss/duplication, concurrent usage loss, failed forced-flush dirty-state loss, durable audit-state eviction, tail replay duplication, and stale compaction slicing. Rebase at the owning Goal X seams: use an item-and-byte-bounded global window, exact on-demand per-goal streaming facts, bounded terminal summaries, bounded checkpoints, and fixed-cardinality mutation tests; preserve concurrent accounting and retry semantics. Until then, sources/pi.json buildPackage=pi-goal-x must cause 0.27.4 to be rejected, rolled back, and held back without aborting the full update.

* DONE [#C] Make the VPS Gitea credential helper read-only
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-6a3
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-12 Wed 19:54]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-12 Wed 19:57]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-12 Wed 19:57]
:CLOSE_REASON: Replaced the writable credential-store callback with a read-only managed helper and verified live private Git access with empty stderr.
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

The VPS host-scoped Git helper uses credential-store directly against a SOPS-managed 0440 file. Authentication succeeds, but Git then invokes store and credential-store emits a fatal lock warning because the immutable managed leaf cannot be rewritten. Replace it with a read-only helper that delegates only get to credential-store and accepts store/erase without mutating the secret.

** Acceptance Criteria

A real private ls-remote through the managed helper succeeds with empty stderr; the SOPS credential file remains root-owned and non-writable; build and authorized activation pass; Git authentication still succeeds after activation.

:CLOSE_REASON: Resolved operational contention and established a serialized baseline. After all competing evaluators drained, the original make/sudo/nix chain exited. A sole offline dirty-tree Hera drvPath evaluation completed in 100.90s (44.86s user, 20.93s sys); clean HEAD completed in 88.59s (46.72s user, 18.52s sys); an immediate warm dirty rerun still took 79.33s (44.47s user, 18.06s sys) without eval-cache SQLite busy. Debug evidence showed cache-hit derivation instantiation/addToStore traffic, no fetch or build. The order-of-magnitude delay was concurrent evaluator/store contention; the underlying cold dirty evaluation remains an 80-100s baseline and must be serialized.
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

A live make switch on Hera left its root nix evaluator at zero CPU for more than twelve minutes. Concurrent bounded probes reproduced eval-cache-v6 SQLite busy errors and heavy daemon addToStore derivation-instantiation traffic with no fetch or build; clean HEAD and the dirty tree both exceed sixty seconds while flake metadata is fast.

** Acceptance Criteria

All competing evaluators are drained; one serialized build-only Hera system evaluation completes or yields a precise non-contention bottleneck; the ordinary single-evaluator path no longer reports eval-cache SQLite busy; timing and process evidence distinguish evaluation from build and activation; no broad store scan or lock deletion is used.

* DONE [#D] Move the wiggum halt-request condition out of the ALL-must-hold exit list
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-84t
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-11 Tue 22:14]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-12 Wed 01:41]
:ASSIGNEE: johnw
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-12 Wed 01:41]
:CLOSE_REASON: Moved stop/halt into an explicit override and added a managed-skill regression assertion.
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

Source commit: b46c837ce2590b56fc72441b0565b00cc33123a9. Severity: Low. Category: Documentation. File: config/ai/skills/wiggum/SKILL.md:28. Confidence: 85.

PROBLEM: The exit-criteria list is prefixed 'Exit the loop ONLY when ALL of these hold', and the commit adds 'No request to stop or halt has been received from the user' as one of those conditions. Read literally, a received halt request makes the condition false, so the ONLY-when-ALL rule forbids exiting — the inverse of the intent. Once a halt arrives, no legitimate exit path exists under the rule as written.

IMPACT: Wiggum drives long autonomous loops where literal instruction-following matters most. A literalist agent that receives 'please halt' mid-loop gets contradictory guidance and may rationalize continuing ('exit criteria are not met'), which is precisely the failure the line was added to prevent.

SUGGESTED FIX: Remove the bullet from the ALL list and state it as an override above the list, e.g.: 'A user request to stop or halt overrides everything below: acknowledge it, record loop state in the handoff, and stop immediately.' The completion criteria then describe successful completion only.

VERIFICATION: Re-read the exit section as a decision procedure with 'halt received' true: the procedure should terminate the loop immediately rather than block exit.

** Notes

Partner-cleanup review confirmed the finding and the minimal two-file fix. The stop/halt override and focused catalog assertion are implemented and verified; no renderer change was needed. Review reports: /private/tmp/wg-cleanup-nix84t-20260812/report.md and /private/tmp/wg-nix84t-cleanup-final/report.md.

* DONE [#C] Narrow Andoria remote-builder root authority                       :builder:security:
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-89t
:LABELS:   ["builder","security"]
:ISSUE_TYPE: task
:CREATED:  [2026-08-12 Wed 12:25]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-13 Thu 02:08]
:ASSIGNEE: johnw
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-13 Thu 02:04]
:CLOSE_REASON: Installed and behaviorally proved the shared root-only signed-import trust policy on both Andoria builders; activated pinned plain ssh-ng rows on Hera and Clio; verified exact managed machines files, healthy daemons, no sudo remote-program, and fresh x86_64-linux dispatch. Human sudo policy was unchanged.
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

Make Andoria-08 a plain ssh-ng builder without elevating the SSH account to a trusted Nix user. The shared nix-config trust record owns require-sigs, trusted-users=root, cache trust, and the authorized client signing public key; johnw/andoria renders that data as one Determinate Nix custom-policy leaf for explicit root installation. Builder transport, human sudo, and ordinary SSH access remain separate authorities.

** Acceptance Criteria

The shared trust record is rendered and installed byte-for-byte on Andoria-08 with require-sigs=true and trusted-users=root; the daemon is restarted; a fresh client-signed ordinary path imports while a fresh unsigned ordinary path is rejected; Hera and Clio use a pinned plain ssh-ng builder URI with no sudo remote-program; a fresh x86_64-linux build runs through that builder; focused serialization, system build, publication, and authorized client activation pass. Human sudo policy is unchanged.

** Notes

The trust leaf is installed and behaviorally proven on both Andoria servers. Publication and both Darwin client activations are complete. Hera rendered three remote rows and Clio four (including Hera); both exact managed files contain the pinned plain Andoria identities, one-job limits, and no sudo remote-program. Fresh default dispatch through Andoria-T2 passed after Andoria-08 failed to open its SSH master; the earlier fresh build through Andoria-08 was proven before that separate host outage.

* DONE [#C] Replace the retired Nix builder with maintained Linux builders
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-8gs
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-12 Wed 11:05]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-12 Wed 14:08]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-12 Wed 14:08]
:CLOSE_REASON: Replaced the retired builder with exact Vulcan and Andoria-08 routes and proved both with fresh local inputs.
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

The live builder configuration historically listed retired ssh-ng://root@nix-builder and a broad Vulcan entry. The declarative owner is config/darwin.nix. Per owner direction, remove the retired VM; use a viable Andoria-08 or Andoria-T2 route for x86_64-linux, while retaining Vulcan only for aarch64-linux. Live probes showed Andoria-T2 cannot accept client-signed local outputs, so the active pool selects Andoria-08 and records T2 remediation separately. Never edit /etc/nix/machines directly; activation is separately authorized.

** Acceptance Criteria

Hera and Clio evaluate to Vulcan as aarch64-linux only plus Andoria-08 as the selected x86_64-linux builder; each newly configured Linux route accepts a fresh uniquely signed local dependency with its managed identity; behavioral evaluation pins host, platform, user, key, capacity, features, ordering, serialization, and daemon handoff; the system builds without activation; no nix-builder or Andoria-T2 entry remains in the final generated configuration. Adoption of the final source is separately tracked by nix-z9h.

:LABELS:   []

:CLOSE_REASON: Dropped with the unsolicited profile-migration/profile-scoped side-store expansion. Do not continue hardening machinery that is no longer an accepted product direction.
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

promoteInboxToInflightLocked validates and fsyncs an opened source inbox, closes it, then hard-links the source pathname. A same-owner replacement in that validation-to-link window can publish an unvalidated inode into the inflight WAL.

** Acceptance Criteria

Keep the validated source descriptor pinned across publication; after linking, reopen the target no-follow and require the exact source inode, regular-file type, owner, and private mode before admission. Any mismatch removes and durably syncs the rejected target without touching the attacker victim or source. Deterministic after-validation pathname-swap, symlink, mode, owner-seam, retry, and normal promotion tests kill pathname-only linking while preserving target-before-source crash ordering.

:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-b47b
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-13 Thu 16:22]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-13 Thu 22:00]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-13 Thu 22:00]
:CLOSE_REASON: Dropped with the unsolicited profile-migration/profile-scoped side-store expansion. Do not continue hardening machinery that is no longer an accepted product direction.
:SOURCE_REPO: agent-deck
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/Products/agent-deck-runtime-bindings-cleanup
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

Agent Deck DeliverCompletion discarded commitEventToInbox transient classification, so ReplayUnackedCompletions could count transient filesystem or SQLite failures toward terminal dead-letter and acknowledgement. Permanent-failure handling also was not acknowledgement-retry-safe: after the dead-letter threshold crossed, an AckCompletion failure left later replays seeing the sink as already logged and never retrying acknowledgement.

** Acceptance Criteria

Transient classification reaches replay policy; transient failures remain retryable without advancing terminal attempt/dead-letter state; permanent failures retain bounded handling; once terminal evidence is durable, acknowledgement retries without advancing attempts or duplicating dead letters; filesystem, SQLite, and acknowledgement-failure regressions cover each state transition.

* DONE [#B] Propagate durable parent-directory sync failures for inbox writes
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-d5f7
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-13 Thu 16:16]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-13 Thu 22:00]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-13 Thu 22:00]
:CLOSE_REASON: Dropped with the unsolicited profile-migration/profile-scoped side-store expansion. Do not continue hardening machinery that is no longer an accepted product direction.
:SOURCE_REPO: agent-deck
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/Products/agent-deck-runtime-bindings-cleanup
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

Agent Deck WriteInboxEvent fsyncs the file but does not durably sync a newly created parent entry, and the shared writeFileDurable primitive swallows directory-sync failures outside the migration-specific strict wrapper. A successful return can therefore precede durable namespace publication. Acceptance: new inbox/state-file creation and replacement propagate file and parent-directory sync errors, crash ordering remains lossless, and fault-injection regressions cover create and replace paths without weakening migration semantics.

:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0

The schema-20 lazy importer currently routes legacy consumed-turn JSON through unbounded readStateFileNoFollow. A corrupt or hostile multi-gigabyte file can exhaust memory before fail-closed decoding. Add a consumed-ledger-specific no-follow regular-file cap that accommodates the 25k/14-day envelope, rejects oversize input without importing/removing it, and retains the inbox WAL.

* DONE [#C] Give Agent Deck GUI descendants a stable TCC responsibility identity
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-e5mi
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-13 Thu 16:07]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-13 Thu 22:09]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-13 Thu 22:01]
:CLOSE_REASON: Dropped as speculative TCC-labeling product scope; the initiating process/prompt premise was unproved and the native supervisor added 381 net lines without preventing filesystem traversal.
:SOURCE_REPO: agent-deck
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/Products/agent-deck-runtime-bindings-cleanup
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

macOS TCC can attribute access checks from GUI helpers and command descendants to an ad-hoc-signed Nix Python responsibility identity. The UI can therefore misleadingly report that Python 3.14.6 is requesting access. Current-boot evidence includes pinentry-related UI checks and, at 14:19:41, 23 indirect File Provider domain checks whose subject was the same Nix Python 3.14.6 executable used by Agent Deck processes. The retained record does not expose the originating Python PID or independently label those 23 checks as Photos, Media Library, or removable-volume services. Stable responsibility identity must not grant broader access or conceal the initiating command.

** Acceptance Criteria

GUI helpers and commands launched through Agent Deck are attributed to a stable, intentional responsible identity rather than generic Python, without broad TCC grants. Focused macOS evidence distinguishes the initiating command, requested TCC service or File Provider domain, responsible process, and actual filesystem access. Pinentry remains secure.

* DONE [#B] Make last-wins inbox replacement failure-atomic
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-ecpl
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-13 Thu 18:35]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-13 Thu 22:00]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-13 Thu 22:00]
:CLOSE_REASON: Dropped with the unsolicited profile-migration/profile-scoped side-store expansion. Do not continue hardening machinery that is no longer an accepted product direction.
:SOURCE_REPO: agent-deck
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/Products/agent-deck-runtime-bindings-cleanup
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

The inbox last-wins path removes the existing child record before opening/appending/syncing its replacement. Any append, write, or sync failure can leave neither old nor new durable state; current tests use different children and do not exercise same-child replacement failure.

** Acceptance Criteria

Last-wins replacement builds one complete image and publishes it with one atomic replace. Open, temp-write, and file-sync failures before rename leave exactly the old child event and are retryable. A post-rename parent-sync error is reported as commit-uncertain; live/crash state may be the whole old image or whole new image, never missing/partial, and retrying the same event converges to exactly one new child event. Same-child fault-injection tests kill delete-before-append ordering and verify bystander/raw-line preservation.

Portable rename has an unavoidable commit-uncertain boundary; rollback adds new fallible mutations and does not solve power loss. Use a single complete-image atomic replace, preserve old on every pre-rename failure, invalidate caches after rename, and prove idempotent retry convergence after directory-sync uncertainty.",

The NFS-shared Home Manager checkout now pins nix-config and config/ai to ssh://gitea@gitea/johnw/nix-config.git, but andoria-t2, delphi-3bd4, and gpu-server resolve gitea to the LAN-only 192.168.1.2:2222 endpoint and fail with No route to host during evaluation. The failed switch also leaves shells reporting a missing ~/.nix-profile/bin/starship. Recover the authorized rollout from the already-built exact activation closure without reevaluation, then establish one durable shared-work route to the canonical Gitea authority so future evaluation needs no ad hoc GIT_SSH_COMMAND or proxy.

** Acceptance Criteria

The exact existing activation closure is proven resident and activated in the required order without another evaluation; starship resolves from the active profile on each host; ordinary locked evaluation can fetch canonical Gitea from every active shared-work host without ad hoc command environment; no GitHub fallback, copied checkout, or raw home-manager/master command is introduced; rollback generations are retained.

* DONE [#B] Namespace hook cost events and watcher consumption by profile
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-emgi
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-13 Thu 15:46]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-13 Thu 18:28]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-13 Thu 18:28]
:CLOSE_REASON: Profile-scoped cost artifacts now have durable ack-after-commit consumption, startup rescan, overwrite-safe identity, and two-profile database coverage.
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

Authority-scoped hook handling emits cost usage to a global profile-less event path. Every profile TUI watches that directory, attributes the event to its own DB, and the first watcher deletes it, allowing same-ID multi-profile mischarge or loss. Carry the validated canonical profile in the cost event identity/path, admit only the matching watcher, and add concurrent duplicate-ID/profile delivery tests.

** Notes

Current-byte independent re-audit confirms profile-scoped paths/IDs/admission, but end-to-end durability remains blocked: CostEventWatcher deletes after channel enqueue before SQLite persistence, main discards WriteCostEvent errors, and watcher Start never scans preexisting artifacts. Crash/transient DB failure/channel-full or daemon-before-TUI events can therefore be lost or stranded. Assigned fix must retain artifacts until successful/idempotent DB commit, rescan startup state, and prove duplicate-ID/profile isolation through two actual profile DBs.

* DONE [#B] Require durable final task records before completion delivery
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-fc5x
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-13 Thu 16:40]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-13 Thu 18:28]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-13 Thu 18:28]
:CLOSE_REASON: Final task record persistence is required before delivery/acknowledgement, and the sabotage/no-delivery command regression passes on final bytes.
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

Agent Deck runtask delivery still proceeds when RunTaskWorker returns an error writing the final completion record. The in-memory result may be delivered and acknowledged while durable storage contains only the pending claim; if the parent is unavailable, the terminal result can be lost, and if delivery succeeds it can acknowledge stale pending state.

** Acceptance Criteria

Final completion-record persistence must succeed before delivery or acknowledgement begins. A failed final write surfaces as task failure, preserves retryable ownership state, performs no terminal delivery/acknowledgement, and a fault-injection regression proves the result cannot be lost or acknowledged against a pending claim.

* DONE [#D] Guard optAgent requests against feed-absent typos
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-gk36
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: task
:CREATED:  [2026-08-14 Fri 18:25]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-16 Sun 11:49]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-16 Sun 11:41]
:CLOSE_REASON: Centralized managed-agent selection in flake/ai.nix, exported optAgent through the portable contract, and made config/packages.nix delegate to it. Names absent on one system remain optional, while names absent from every supported feed now fail with the exact name. Added a Home Manager mutant using claude-code-acp and portable-lib contract coverage. Verification: direct mutant eval failed with claude-code-acp; managed-agent-package-selection built; config/ai all-system no-build evaluation passed; full make test passed (21 Python suites, 500s, zero failures/timeouts, plus all seven behavioral checks).
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

Source commit: d0abf4fd726149c1f21545fbc2d1e6b22b8c9b52. Severity: Low. Category: Test Coverage. File: config/packages.nix:515 and flake/ai.nix optAgent. Confidence: 85.

PROBLEM: optAgent deliberately returns [] for names absent from the feed (feeds differ per system), so a misspelled agent name installs nothing with no diagnostic. This just happened: 73fcbeaf requested 'claude-code-acp', which exists in no feed; the managed-agent-package-selection and host-behavior gates both passed with the typo, and only manual inspection caught it (corrected to 'claude-agent-acp' in d0abf4fd 12 minutes later).

IMPACT: A typo'd or upstream-renamed agent silently vanishes from every home while all gates stay green — the same silent-inventory-drift class the nix-scripts exact cmp and the source-project input allowlist already guard elsewhere.

SUGGESTED FIX: Add a check (or extend managed-agent-package-selection) asserting every name passed to optAgent resolves in at least one supported system's feed — absent-on-some-systems stays legal, absent-everywhere fails as a typo. A literal expected-agents list compared two-way against the requested names would match the house inventory pattern.

VERIFICATION: Re-introduce 'claude-code-acp' as a mutant; the check must fail naming the unresolvable agent; with correct names it passes on all systems.

Stage without preserved metadata, clear inherited ACLs on Darwin, force 0600, and test both POSIX mode and ACL absence before side effects.

* DONE [#B] Fix the codex non-darwin open-file-limit case broken by the hostPlatform sweep
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-ki4n
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-14 Fri 10:40]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-14 Fri 11:21]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-14 Fri 11:21]
:CLOSE_REASON: Corrected the non-Darwin wrapper fixture to override stdenv.hostPlatform.isDarwin; the focused agent-wrappers derivation now passes.
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

Source commit: f865ddbba80f49e849c066602feaa7f4af434d79. Severity: High. Category: Bug. File: test/ai/agent-wrappers.sh (case codex/72-non-darwin-limit) with flake/ai/wrappers/codex.nix. Confidence: 100.

PROBLEM: The stdenv.isDarwin -> stdenv.hostPlatform.isDarwin sweep breaks the BASELINE agent-wrappers check. Reproduced twice on the identical derivation, including from a clean worktree of the commit itself:

  nix build .#checks.aarch64-darwin.agent-wrappers
  -> agent-wrappers check [.../codex/72-non-darwin-limit]:
     missing upstream environment entry: AGENT_TEST_OPEN_FILE_LIMIT=256
  (drv /nix/store/ikgqmsvh2a6026c6wbb6h2hxn6k2g4rs-agent-wrappers-check.drv)

The non-darwin case exercises the wrapper generated under a Linux hostPlatform instantiation. Under the old build-platform predicates that wrapper still embedded the darwin branch (buildPlatform is darwin on this machine); with hostPlatform predicates it now genuinely embeds the non-darwin branch, and the case's expectation (an AGENT_TEST_OPEN_FILE_LIMIT=256 entry reaching the fake upstream) no longer holds — either the expectation was calibrated against the old mis-branched wrapper, or the wrapper's non-darwin limit handling needs the entry it no longer produces.

IMPACT: agent-wrappers is a baseline behavioral check: make test, nix flake check, the closeout tier, and CI are red at HEAD.

SUGGESTED FIX: Decide which side is right for a real Linux host: if the non-darwin wrapper must preserve/propagate the caller's open-file limit to upstream, restore that in flake/ai/wrappers/codex.nix under the hostPlatform predicate; if the old expectation encoded the mis-branched behavior, update case 72's expected environment to the correct non-darwin contract. Keep the wrapper and case in one commit.

VERIFICATION: nix build .#checks.aarch64-darwin.agent-wrappers succeeds; case 72 exercises the Linux-hostPlatform wrapper text (grep the generated script for the limit branch) so the case provably tests the non-darwin artifact.

* DONE [#B] Fix Hermes native SearXNG TLS trust
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-koys
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-14 Fri 00:43]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-14 Fri 01:00]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-14 Fri 01:00]
:CLOSE_REASON: Fixed at Vulcan authority in signed commit 0951e14b: hermes-agent exports the Vulcan-aware CA through SSL_CERT_FILE, the packaged-httpx env-i TLS regression passed, and full build-only realization produced rhaansni6zj0big7g4gxjfd71h9yii8m. No switch, restart, or activation occurred.
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

Hermes MicroVM native web_search uses packaged Python/httpx against https://searxng.vulcan.lan. The host SearXNG/uWSGI/nginx service and the Node mcp-searxng adapter are healthy, but the hermes-agent service exports SEARXNG_URL without SSL_CERT_FILE. Exact packaged Python under env -i reproduces CERTIFICATE_VERIFY_FAILED; adding SSL_CERT_FILE=/etc/ssl/certs/ca-certificates.crt returns HTTP 200 with results. Fix at Vulcan's authoritative /etc/nixos Hermes service environment, not in the root Node MCP wrapper. Add an evaluation assertion and an env-i behavioral TLS trust regression. No service restart or activation is authorized by this issue.

* DONE [#C] Avoid repeated pinentry prompts from isolated Ares publication signing homes
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-ky90
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-13 Thu 16:07]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-13 Thu 18:06]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-13 Thu 18:06]
:CLOSE_REASON: Resolved as a non-source operational incident: no maintained Ares/Nix signer coordinator exists to patch without adding dead code. The repository agent contract now requires one workflow-scoped isolated signer home/agent across bounded steps, separate keyless verifiers, exact provenance, teardown, and no login-agent mutation; the contract is regression-pinned and the offline catalog check passes. Any future maintained coordinator must add the executable two-transaction prompt-count probe beside its implementation.
:SOURCE_REPO: ares
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/hera/ares/main
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

Two Ares publication preflight runs created separate temporary GPG homes and gpg-agent processes. Each detached-signature operation therefore missed the normal long-lived passphrase cache and launched pinentry-mac independently. Preserve signing isolation and signature verification, but avoid redundant interactive authorization within one publication workflow.

** Acceptance Criteria

A bounded publication workflow that performs repeated preflight/signing steps requires no more interactive pinentry prompts than cryptographically necessary; isolated verification homes remain keyless; signature provenance and fail-closed behavior are preserved; a regression test or executable acceptance probe covers two sequential signing transactions.

Ares promotion_preflight does not sign, and Nix bin/publish only verifies existing commit signatures. Root cause is allocating a distinct GNUPGHOME and gpg-agent for each transaction, which necessarily creates separate passphrase caches despite the canonical login agent already having maximal TTLs. Durable contract: one private workflow-scoped signer home/agent reused only across the bounded sequential transactions, exact signer fingerprint and payload/revision/transaction binding, separate keyless verifier homes, fail-closed verification, teardown on success/failure, and no cross-workflow or ambient-agent mutation. Andoria-T2 has the shared minimal Determinate Nix trust leaf installed: require-sigs remains true, trusted-users remains root, and the exact authorized client signing public key is trusted. Signed-positive and unsigned-negative imports were proven. Its pinned plain ssh-ng row is published and activated on both Hera and Clio; exact managed-file readback and fresh default x86_64-linux dispatch through T2 passed. Human sudo and root SSH are unchanged.

** Acceptance Criteria

The authoritative Andoria-T2 system configuration declaratively grants only the minimum Nix builder authority or trusts the exact client signing key; a fresh nonce-bearing local output is consumed by an x86_64-linux derivation through ssh-ng; no broad sudo or root SSH is added; Hera and Clio then re-add the exact pinned T2 identity with behavioral and serialized-file coverage.

Hera configuration evaluation still takes 40-200 seconds on serialized focused runs. make switch and bin/update also hand the flake back to sudo darwin-rebuild, causing root to repeat the full evaluation with a separate cache. The prior nix-s45q work removed obvious duplicate package-set construction and disproved a Hera-versus-Clio asymmetry, but did not establish that the remaining shared evaluation cost is irreducible.

** Acceptance Criteria

The maintained focused Hera evaluation path completes within 30 seconds on a serialized repeatable benchmark without weakening configuration checks or behavior. The Darwin switch workflow evaluates and builds the system closure exactly once as the invoking user, validates that closure, and elevates only the system-profile update and activation of that already-built closure. Focused regression tests protect the privilege and failure-ordering boundary. No activation is required without separate authorization.

* DONE [#D] Designate vps-push as a bash recipe in the Makefile
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-n7vb
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-13 Thu 23:36]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-14 Fri 00:15]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-14 Fri 00:15]
:CLOSE_REASON: Completed: vps-push now uses the existing target-local Bash authority; behavioral Make regression proves it ignores a rejecting global shell, and focused 9-test Make safety plus Ruff and dry expansion pass.
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

Source commit: 852f482bb82bf5726c12b07814cb065ca1e36658. Severity: Low. Category: Bug. File: Makefile:362. Confidence: 90.

PROBLEM: The new vps-push recipe begins 'set -euo pipefail' but is not listed on the Makefile's 'SHELL := bash' designation line (line 23), whose comment exists precisely to mark strict-mode recipes. 'pipefail' is not POSIX sh: it works on macOS because /bin/sh is bash in sh mode, but fails immediately ('Illegal option -o pipefail') under dash, the /bin/sh on Debian/Ubuntu hosts. It is the only pipefail outside the designated set.

IMPACT: On any Linux invocation the target dies before doing anything (fail-closed, so no misbuild) — but the deeper point is the pipefail here is load-bearing: the sign pipeline 'nix-store --query ... | grep -v .drv | xargs nix store sign' would otherwise mask a failed requisites query behind grep/xargs success, and losing strict mode silently would degrade exactly the signing guarantee the commit message explains was worth two failed builds to get right.

SUGGESTED FIX: Add vps-push to line 23: 'verify-inputs update-projects upgrade-tasks changes copy travel-ready vps-push: SHELL := bash'.

VERIFICATION: 'make -n vps-push' unchanged; on a dash-sh system (or 'make SHELL=dash vps-push' dry probe) the recipe no longer aborts at set; grep confirms every pipefail recipe is bash-designated.

  {
    "id": 339,
    "issue_id": "nix-nct6",
    "author": "johnw",
    "text": "2026-08-13 deeper current-byte audit BLOCKER. RunTaskWorker writes a pending source claim under the short record lock, runs the task command without the migration authority, then writes final completion and ledger back to its original profile. Migration can move the pending claim while the command runs; the later final writer reacquires the old source lock and resurrects terminal source state while target retains stale pending state. Existing writer-vs-migration test barriers only inside one WriteCompletionRecord and force writer-first ordering. * DEFER [#A] Enforce an eight-CPU ceiling on shared-work Nix builds
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-p17
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-13 Thu 00:23]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-14 Fri 02:38]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

The shared-work Home Manager profile and Darwin clients requested one job with eight cores, but that policy was only cooperative. During the retained lock update, a GPU Server derivation ignored NIX_BUILD_CORES and the nixbld cgroup consumed approximately 7.7 to 9.6 logical CPUs across five samples. Every reachable shared-work nix-daemon.service initially had access to the host's full CPU set, so the configuration did not satisfy the owner requirement that Nix never use more than eight logical CPUs on these four machines.

Keep max-jobs=1 and cores=8 as useful scheduler/build-system hints, but enforce the literal execution boundary with systemd AllowedCPUs=0-7 on nix-daemon.service. Declare the CPU set once in the existing shared host-capacity authority, render it through a separate Andoria data-only package, and apply the root-owned drop-in independently on each active shared-work host. Keep the Andoria cache/signature root-policy output unchanged. Do not create a deployment framework or conflate human sudo policy with Nix resource policy.

The interrupted GPU build produced no activation path and was terminated cleanly before this correction; do not retry it until the hard CPU set is active.

** Acceptance Criteria

The shared root authority declares one CPU set for shared-work Nix daemons. The authoritative Andoria consumer renders a separate systemd drop-in with AllowedCPUs=0-7 without a deployment framework or trust-policy change; max-jobs=1 and cores=8 remain scheduler guidance. The same shared-work package checkpoint proves Watchman absent, Agda absent on shared-work hosts, Agda retained on personal and Darwin hosts, and no duplicate Andoria name filter. On andoria-08, andoria-t2, delphi-3bd4, and gpu-server, the installed drop-in byte-matches the rendered source, systemd and the live cgroup report CPUs 0-7, and an affected builder reports Cpus_allowed_list 0-7. One full shared-work activationPackage build and one authorized ordered four-host activation satisfy the merged package and CPU outcomes.

* DONE [#B] Open sandbox bridge profile storage writable without migration
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-p598
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-13 Thu 14:31]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-13 Thu 14:50]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-13 Thu 14:50]
:CLOSE_REASON: Existing profile storage now opens writable, context-bounded, without creation/migration; bridge refresh and Stop finalization regressions pass.
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

OpenExistingProfileStorageContext currently returns a read-only SQLite handle, but Context status refresh and Stop consumed-turn finalization perform durable writes through it. This makes live refresh/finalize fail or emit stale state. Add a context-bounded existing-profile read-write opener that does not create directories or run migrations; use it for bridge requests and prove deadline, no-auto-create, status refresh, and Stop finalization.

:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-p5mw
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-13 Thu 15:43]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-13 Thu 15:51]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-13 Thu 15:51]
:CLOSE_REASON: Adjudicated as a harness error: GOSUMDB=off is incompatible with verified auto-toolchain selection. Product intentionally pins go1.25.12; replacement full lane will use root direnv, existing module/build caches, GOPROXY=off, and normal cached checksum verification.
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

The first full-suite command forced GOSUMDB=off while TestSmoke_BuildVersion intentionally launches the exact go.mod toolchain (go1.25.12). Go refuses toolchain-module verification when the checksum database is disabled even if the payload is cached. This is a verification-harness error, not a product source defect: run the outer command through the root direnv with explicit existing GOMODCACHE/GOCACHE and leave GOSUMDB at its normal verified setting; retain the pinned smoke assertion.

Source commit: 29d59060a9f7 (Update project pins). Severity: High. Category: Bug. File: test/ai/pi-copy-message.check.ts. Confidence: 100.

PROBLEM: Pi Copy Message 2.0.0 newly imports decodeKittyPrintable from @earendil-works/pi-tui. The pinned Pi TUI does export that symbol, but the focused Bun test fully replaces the module with mock.module and omitted the new named export. The Gallery check therefore failed during test module loading before exercising Copy Message.

IMPACT: pi-gallery is a baseline behavioral check, so the accepted pin sweep left make test and the update closeout red. The defect is in the independently maintained test double, not the packaged Pi runtime.

FIX: Extend only the existing pi-tui mock with a no-op decodeKittyPrintable export. Keep the real pinned module unchanged. The tested latest, numeric-selection, and copy-user paths do not invoke interactive Kitty decoding.

VERIFICATION: Build the exact aarch64-darwin pi-gallery check and prove the Copy Message suite plus Gallery smoke pass against the pinned Pi package.

:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-qkv5
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-13 Thu 15:43]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-13 Thu 22:00]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-13 Thu 22:00]
:CLOSE_REASON: Dropped with the unsolicited profile-migration/profile-scoped side-store expansion. Do not continue hardening machinery that is no longer an accepted product direction.
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

Agent Deck runtime hook authority and sandbox bridge artifact paths were keyed only by instance ID even though duplicate IDs across profiles are supported. Profile-qualified paths now isolate artifacts, but production still starts only one profile-bound sandbox bridge server while the transition daemon services every profile, so non-daemon-profile request trees are not watched and time out. Key every authority/bridge artifact by canonical profile plus instance ID, fail closed on legacy state, and supervise bridge service for every profile handled by the daemon.

** Acceptance Criteria

Duplicate instance IDs across profiles cannot collide in authority, hook status, request, response, acknowledgement, capacity, or cleanup paths. The notify daemon discovers and services every active profile request tree, including profiles created after startup, with bounded supervised watcher failure behavior. Legacy state fails closed or migrates safely. Focused duplicate-profile and real Start-plus-Exchange tests prove requests for non-default profiles complete rather than timing out.

The canonical Agent Deck product repository and branch are confirmed on Gitea; the worktree origin fetch/push URLs use only that authority; no GitHub push URL remains; the reviewed signed product commit is published only after separate explicit push authorization; the Nix source pin fetches the immutable reviewed product revision from the approved authority or a documented release mirror; no client relies on an unpublished object.

2026-08-13 read-only authority probe: neither gitea@gitea:johnw/agent-deck.git nor gitea@gitea:jwiegley/agent-deck.git exists or is readable. The product worktree remains configured with GitHub origin; no remote was changed and no push was attempted. Creating/selecting the Gitea product repository is an external mutation requiring explicit authority before publication.

:CLOSE_REASON: Pinned the reviewed MCR SQL Server 2022 CU26 schema-2 manifest digest in the source catalog; the managed launcher rejects tag-only or truncated references and uses the digest-only reference for pull, bind probe, and final startup. Updated security and current-work documentation, preserved the separate nix-jlj emulation decision, and passed formatting, parsing, source-catalog, root/portable all-system, 92 strict, and focused credential-boundary gates. No image pull, service activation, credential access, or data migration was performed.

:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

The managed MSSQL launcher still uses mcr.microsoft.com/mssql/server:2022-latest. Resolving the pulled tag to one image ID prevents movement between probe and final startup within a launch, but a later launch can receive a retagged artifact that gets the writable database mount and SA credential. MCR currently serves a single linux/amd64 Docker schema-2 manifest, not a multi-architecture index: sha256:ba4c8329f48fb8f02e1416be6a930ebfd71268caee78aa985f3af4315e457c89, also published as 2022-CU26-ubuntu-22.04. Pin that reviewed digest in an appropriate source authority, use the same digest-only reference for pull/probe/final execution, reject tag-only references, and document explicit reviewed updates. Discovered during independent review of nix-tcz.3; official MCR/Microsoft evidence captured under /tmp/wg-mssql-research/.

:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-s60
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug

Source commit: 8d44b0d79a52aeb1334f6984a61d58089803c733. Severity: Medium. Category: Bug. File: config/ai/skills/wiggum/SKILL.md:1. Confidence: 100.

PROBLEM: The commit replaced the YAML frontmatter block (--- name: wiggum / description: ... ---) with a Markdown-formatter artifact: an underscore horizontal rule followed by a single giant '## name: wiggum description: ...' heading. This is the signature of a Markdown reflow tool treating frontmatter as document text. The skill's name/description metadata is no longer parseable frontmatter.

IMPACT: Clients that read SKILL.md frontmatter for the skill listing (name, description, self-invocation guard) get no metadata or a garbled heading; the 'do not self-invoke' description guidance no longer surfaces in skill listings. The body also picked up a typo in the new Working policies section ('alwaoys').

SUGGESTED FIX: Restore the literal frontmatter block delimited by --- lines with the same name/description content, fix 'alwaoys' to 'always', and exclude SKILL.md frontmatter from whatever formatter pass produced this (or teach it to skip YAML frontmatter).

VERIFICATION: head -8 of the file shows --- delimited YAML; the skill listing shows the wiggum description again; grep finds no 'alwaoys'.

handleWatcherInstallSkill writes deterministic SKILL.md and README.md paths with os.WriteFile, which follows a pre-existing symlink in the user-owned skill pool. Enforcing 0644 with path-based chmod would also follow it. Acceptance: create files through no-follow descriptor-based writes, explicitly set 0644 independent of umask, reject hostile symlink/non-regular targets, and prove an external sentinel is unchanged.

* DONE [#A] Remove agent-visible Node-RED runtime-secret access              :security:validated:
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-tcz.1
:LABELS:   ["heavy-review","security","validated"]
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-10 Mon 13:25]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-10 Mon 21:50]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-10 Mon 21:50]
:CLOSE_REASON: Implemented the host-owned three-command Node-RED admin boundary in authoritative Vulcan commits 7a808a186b521299916679a66428299b0698dcfb and c9ad1372f970cbdb52648bbc94e039c07522c662, then aligned the root managed skill, validator, whole-tree secret/API scan, fail-closed workflow, stale-digest envelope, strict acknowledgement, and timeout documentation. Authoritative Vulcan tests passed 128/128 plus all-system evaluation; root behavioral/focused/catalog and root/portable all-system gates passed; independent review is clear after correcting one config-node fixture. External commits remain unpushed and neither repository was activated; live TLS, sudo, secret ownership, service, and command acceptance remain separately authorized runtime checks.
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

Critical. The managed Node-RED skill formerly directed agents to read the runtime admin token and place it in curl argv, contradicting the repository secret boundary. Replace that route with one host-owned helper that reads the transport credential internally, exposes only three bounded flow-author commands, and never places that credential in agent-visible input, argv, output, logs, or generated files. The caller is a trusted authorized flow author: selected flow configuration is bounded but sensitive authorized output, not globally non-secret data. Authentication, diagnostics, concurrent-update protection, validation, and documented workflows must fail closed. Sources: validated VCR-001, deep Q-01, post-commit fess audit.

** Notes

The intended boundary is routine non-disclosure of the helper transport credential for a trusted, authorized flow author; selected flow configuration is bounded but sensitive authorized output, not globally non-secret data. Post-commit fess audit reopened the issue for fail-open Admin API authentication, fixed diagnostic logging, stale selected-flow conflict protection, strict PUT acknowledgement, fail-closed docs, validator compatibility, stale credential-store guidance, and truthful timeout semantics: each network I/O is bounded at 10 seconds while one non-rearmed 15-second deadline covers the whole operation including parsing and output flushing.

:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17

:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-13 Thu 12:53]

:CREATED_BY: codex

:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

loadStopBlockStateLocked currently converts every read or JSON error into the zero-value counter. Zero is the permissive state, so a corrupt or unreadable budget can make every Stop block indefinitely.

** Acceptance Criteria

Only absence initializes an empty Stop budget; I/O, permission, and decode failures surface and force a no-block decision without overwriting the damaged state; regressions cover malformed JSON and injected read failure.

agent-deck-runtime-lifecycle.patch is about 45,582 lines, far beyond the documented narrow compatibility-patch boundary. Import the exact current Agent Deck product tree into the verified owner fork, pin the reviewed immutable product commit in sources/ai.json, and retain only genuinely Nix-specific packaging changes. Prove path, type, bytes, symlink, and executable identity before removing the downstream patch; then run the complete Agent Deck and Nix package checks. Source: abstraction finding 8.

** Acceptance Criteria

This is one indivisible Agent Deck product checkpoint. The owner designates the canonical Gitea repository and branch; no writable GitHub remote remains; any product push is separately authorized and is read back from Gitea. The selected GOOD-signed product commit reproduces the currently shipped downstream composition by an exact path, type, bytes, symlink, and executable manifest. Within the retained runtime scope, RuntimeBindings is the sole mutable runtime authority, and any retained runtime-hook authority path rejects symlinked or unowned ancestors and unsafe fallback resolution. sources/ai.json pins that exact immutable Gitea revision; package tests assert provider, revision, and source identity and fail on an unreviewed revision. The three product-scale patches and their application are deleted only after the exact product and Nix package checks plus one non-activating Hera system build pass. Profile migration, schema-20 consumed-turn optimization, multi-profile side-store expansion, and their derived hardening programmes are explicitly excluded from this checkpoint.

** Notes

2026-08-10 authority audit: no reviewed exact-tree Agent Deck product commit currently exists. The verified destination fork is jwiegley/agent-deck, but its visible tips do not reproduce v1.11.0 plus the current Discord, transition-daemon, and runtime-lifecycle patches. Create an import-only branch from upstream 2f586c5ca97d0e783bb16213d388bcce9e675e67 and require a zero-difference path/type/bytes/symlink/executable manifest before changing the Nix pin. Product pushes or pull requests remain separately authorized. 2026-08-13 scope correction: Pi product migration is owned wholly by nix-tcz.37.1.25 and its bounded-memory programme. This issue is Agent Deck-only, so the owner-approved Pi deferral does not block Agent Deck RuntimeBindings work.

Programme E0. Complete the existing Pi SessionRepo and SessionTree direction so, for fixed active context, budgets, concurrency, and reviewed topology, every source-reviewable live value on the normal history dataflow has a bound independent of total persisted bytes and entries. Preserve exact durable history, branches, forks, resume, and export. Treat the downstream 0.83 patch as a prototype and test corpus, never as a third session architecture. External outreach, pull requests, fork publication, Nix cutover, activation, and publication remain separate authority gates.

** Acceptance Criteria

Every issue in the frozen pi-bounded-memory roster is closed, explicitly deferred, or has an accepted non-goal. For the actual coding-agent path and certified managed consumers, independent review closes a capability-consumer and history-dataflow inventory across roots, synchronous and suspended transient values, exposed dependency buffers, and declared child processes; every normal ingress is bounded before allocation or hydration. Small merge-blocking boundary tests, a four-scale deterministic qualification including an online append and compaction staircase, lifecycle cleanup, and the exact packaged paired-endpoint RSS integration check pass. Exact durable history, branch, fork, export, explicit legacy compatibility, and cold-work reporting remain intact. Product-scale downstream Pi patches are gone, and no result is presented as a formal proof about opaque runtimes, allocators, arbitrary JavaScript, or third-party extensions.

** Notes

Durable continuation, 2026-08-12 PDT. The bounded-history invariant and frozen C1 contract remain unchanged. B1 exact-upstream source, deterministic fixtures, and its sole descriptive low/high measurement are complete; do not rerun the measurement. B1 closed on retained signed GOOD commits e74ee383, 325ba2a, and 295371fd after exact deterministic artifacts, portable/root evaluations, canonical make test, pre-commit, and a fresh independent no-history fess CLEAR. Original measured fixture and evidence authorities remain immutable; corrected deterministic artifacts are not substitute qualification. Nix-tcz.37.1.3 is now the next ready programme issue and remains unclaimed. Obr is the sole continuation authority. Maintainer outreach, PR and fork publication, Nix cutover, activation, push, history rewrite, and fleet acceptance remain separately authorized.

* DONE [#A] Freeze the Pi bounded-session memory contract and evidence ledger :memory:pi:upstream:
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-tcz.37.1.1
:LABELS:   ["memory","pi","pi-bounded-memory","upstream"]
:ISSUE_TYPE: task
:CREATED:  [2026-08-11 Tue 21:40]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-11 Tue 23:59]
:ASSIGNEE: johnw
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-11 Tue 23:58]
:CLOSE_REASON: Signed corrective commit 328cff3f45ece73b8af036a39ef904f905d4ab83 has a GOOD John Wiegley signature and changes only CURRENT-WORK, the bounded-memory contract, and PLAN. Four exact-byte pre-commit reviews were CLEAR; the no-build config/ai flake check and pre-commit suite passed; and a fresh fork_turns=none fess audit returned CLEAR with no findings. No fixture, control, B1, M1, or M2 measurement ran.
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

Programme C1. Freeze the bounded-session-memory claim and evidence boundary before implementation measurement. Define Pi-controlled history independence as a bounded-by-construction property of the exact reviewed core and certified consumers: every normal history ingress is bounded before allocation or hydration, and every source-reviewable root or transient value on that dataflow has a closed owner, bound, and release inventory. Keep deterministic conformance and packaged RSS as validation layers rather than pretending finite samples prove Big-O. Preserve historical prototype evidence without promoting it.

** Acceptance Criteria

The artifact separates the non-formal history-dataflow argument, deterministic conformance, and finite packaged RSS evidence. It defines four logically nested fixtures whose bytes and entry counts both scale, per-fixture exact-output oracles, cross-size equality only for normalized live projections and matched retained occupancy gauges, capped transient peaks, fixed cleanup baselines, and per-fixture cumulative-work oracles. The closed inventory covers synchronous locals, parser and exposed native buffers, suspended state, caller results, certified consumers, relevant dependencies, and every declared process used during the full workload; per-entry IDs, offsets, hashes, and flags are history state. Small boundary fixtures are merge-blocking; M1 and M2 run full four-scale conformance plus an online append and compaction staircase. B1 is a minimal inspected exact-upstream causal baseline. M1 qualifies one realized wrapper and gallery package in fresh closed-world mutable namespaces and two order-balanced 16 MiB and 1 GiB pairs, using the median of every scheduled sample and an absolute raw-RSS difference at most 32 MiB. M2 reuses only a passing M1 under complete behavior-affecting identity equality and otherwise reruns the gate. Evidence uses ordinary Git, Nix, and artifact identities with simple pass, product-failure, and invalid outcomes and an explicit non-formal, non-adversarial boundary.

** Notes

Closed 2026-08-11 PDT on signed GOOD commit 328cff3f45ece73b8af036a39ef904f905d4ab83 after four exact-byte reviews, a green config/ai no-build check, two green pre-commit runs, and a fresh fork_turns=none fess CLEAR. No fixture, control, B1, M1, or M2 measurement ran. B1 is the sole ready issue. This issue records the frozen contract; future protocol changes follow change control. Push, activation, publication, delivery, and history rewrite remain separately authorized.

Programme W4. Run the complete managed gallery and non-gallery Pi consumers with implicit full-history APIs denied before materialization. Exercise histories beyond all configured horizons and records above ordinary page budgets. Give every consumer one final disposition: unchanged certified, migrated certified, or explicit administrative/unsafe exception.

** Acceptance Criteria

No certified extension invokes a legacy materializer or eager legacy hook payload; point, page, active-context, and exact-scan semantics remain correct beyond old windows. For every extension, independent review inventories every source-reviewable root or transient value receiving history-derived data, including synchronous locals, page and stream callbacks, values across await, direct-file results, caches, queues, listeners, timers, and any child process; each has an owner plus item, byte, concurrency, and lifecycle bound. Accumulating IDs, offsets, hashes, flags, or other metadata per historical record fails certification just as payload retention does. Every configured bound has deterministic enforcement evidence; direct-file and administrative exceptions remain visible. Every maintained consumer has a final disposition. Pi Sessions stays excluded and audit-only. M1 owns local packaged qualification and M2 owns final identity verification or replay.

:PROPERTIES:

:CLOSE_REASON: Signed GOOD closeout e74ee383, corrections 325ba2a and 295371fd, exact deterministic artifacts, full gates, and fresh no-history fess CLEAR complete Programme B1. The sole descriptive measurement remains immutable and must not be rerun.
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0

Programme B1. Pin and inspect one exact unmodified upstream coding-agent snapshot, build the shared nested fixtures, and establish the classic eager-retention causal baseline with an empty extension manifest. This is source and descriptive baseline evidence for the maintainer RFC, not qualification of the later strict gallery, Nix wrapper, or production harness.

** Acceptance Criteria

The exact upstream source and package identity is frozen and inspected before any behavioral attribution. Four logically nested 16 MiB, 64 MiB, 256 MiB, and 1 GiB fixtures preserve one normalized active projection while both persisted bytes and entry count increase by at least threefold per step; exact source hashes, bytes, entries, and per-fixture semantic oracles are recorded. Source evidence identifies whether the selected classic path retains complete-history structures such as fileEntries and byId. One fresh low and high diagnostic with an empty extension manifest records raw RSS, heap and external diagnostics, retained classic entry counts, semantic results, commands, and immutable bundle hashes. It is expected to expose history-sized retention; its RSS value cannot satisfy M1 or M2. B1 does not build speculative multiprocess containment or the final product harness.

** Notes

CLOSED. Exact upstream Pi 0.84.1 source, four deterministic fixtures, and the sole descriptive 16 MiB/1 GiB diagnostic are complete; never rerun that measurement. Governing measured fixture authority remains /nix/store/zan7ir6jx415h85my3w07vla2mzvksyw-pi-classic-core-fixtures-v1 and immutable evidence remains /nix/store/cp9sqkmswmxab9aa4hwdj4zz6d4hxvx0-pi-b1-classic-core-attempt-0001. Retained signed GOOD commits e74ee383, 325ba2a, and 295371fd close all fess findings. Final corrected deterministic output /nix/store/f3njxqqf3mq7kgxncg9ysvmhaj8j3j1l-pi-classic-core-fixtures-v1 preserves byte-identical JSONL fixtures and enforces mandatory exact six-fold accounting plus both postflight exit outcomes. Portable/root evaluations, exact fixture/app builds, canonical make test, pre-commit, and fresh exact no-history fess are CLEAR. This evidence is descriptive-only and does not qualify M1 or M2. No activation or push was performed.

Programme A2. Build the merge-blocking deterministic suite and close the capability-consumer and history-dataflow inventory for the exact Pi core and normal runtime path. Every history ingress must cross a bounded capability before allocation or hydration; every source-reviewable root and transient value driven by history, including synchronous locals, parser and exposed native buffers, suspended state, caller results, and declared child processes, must have an owner, item and byte bound, release point, and test. Use shared semantic fixtures, faux providers, and in-memory transports; no network, ports, sleeps, or finalizer-dependent cleanup.

** Acceptance Criteria

Independent review closes a source-located history-dataflow inventory for the exact core and relevant dependency boundaries. Per-entry metadata is treated as history state and unbounded indexes are disk-only. Retained occupancy gauges agree at matched barriers, transient peaks stay within common caps, cleanup gauges return to zero or a fixed baseline, and cumulative work matches per-fixture expectations. Small limit, limit-plus-one, multi-page, branch, cancellation, replacement, and cleanup fixtures are merge-blocking. The full qualification additionally runs all four fixture sizes, exact tree, fork, and streaming export oracles, cold streaming import or rebuild, and an online append and compaction staircase under fixed active context. Strict paths reject whole-history allocation before hydration. Eager payload and compact per-entry-metadata mutations invalidate the inventory even below the RSS tolerance. Failure atomicity, slow-consumer backpressure, cancellation, and resource cleanup pass on required runtimes; no assertion is weakened to meet wall time.
