Programme M1. After the exact history-dataflow inventory, deterministic product gate, certified gallery, and reproducible H1 package and harness are complete, qualify one realized local Nix package containing the actual Pi wrapper, complete gallery, faux provider, and fixed normal workload. This is the pre-delivery product result.

** Acceptance Criteria

Independent review closes the capability-consumer and history-dataflow inventory for the exact product source, certified gallery, relevant dependencies, configuration, budgets, and complete workload topology. At all four sizes, fixture-specific exact-output oracles pass; normalized live projections and retained occupancy gauges agree; transient peaks remain capped; cleanup reaches its fixed baseline; cumulative work matches per-fixture expectations; and the online append and compaction staircase plateaus under fixed active context. The H1 harness and analyzer controls pass. The packaged RSS check uses fresh closed-world state and process sets in two adjacent endpoint pairs, one 16 MiB then 1 GiB and one 1 GiB then 16 MiB. Each endpoint summary is the median of every scheduled post-barrier sample, and both absolute aggregate raw-RSS differences are at most 32 MiB, with no subtraction, reserves, sample omission, or outlier deletion. Host monitoring brackets each pair; proven ambient or harness faults are invalid, while candidate-caused resource pressure is a product failure. One cold diagnostic per size reports peak, latency, I/O, and disk separately. Only a passing immutable bundle qualifies M1; it records complete product, configuration, inventory, fixture, harness, runtime, topology, host, raw-record, hash, and next-action identity in obr.

** Notes

OPEN and blocked by A2, K1, H1, and the other explicit open graph dependencies. B1 is closed and its dependency edge is satisfied; its descriptive evidence is complete and must not be rerun. No M1 run has begun. The reviewed history-dataflow inventory is the architectural basis; deterministic tests validate every bound; paired RSS is a finite material-regression backstop. Do not restore multiple descriptive lanes, statistical bootstrap, reserve pools, or campaign transparency machinery.

** Acceptance Criteria

Local documentation says source-reviewable Pi-controlled live session state is bounded by construction for fixed active context, budgets, concurrency, and reviewed topology, including transient operation state and long-running append behavior. It explicitly places opaque runtime and allocator internals outside that non-formal source argument and does not claim total RSS is mathematically constant or that finite samples prove Big-O. Legacy whole-history calls state that invocation forfeits the bound. Recipes cover point lookup, recent page, active context, exact reduction, file-backed high-cardinality results, cross-session scans, and extension-owned logs. Documentation distinguishes architectural review, deterministic conformance, finite packaged RSS, historical evidence, and current obr status. No remote publication occurs in this issue.

Programme H1. After maintainers select the authoritative repositories and architecture, build a development-only local-source package and the content-addressed product qualification harness. The package must use the production Node runtime, immutable gallery, wrapper, and behavioral checks without committing local paths or mutating source locks. N1 remains the sole production pin owner.

** Acceptance Criteria

Two clean checkouts at the same commits produce the same package tree and test inputs; dirty or identity-mismatched trees fail closed. The harness runs the exact realized wrapper and gallery with a faux provider and gives every endpoint a fresh mode-0700 closed-world mutable namespace covering home, Pi and XDG roots, temporary and working directories, store, extension, cache, database, lock, socket, service, and helper state. Candidate-owned mutable access outside it or attachment to ambient services is denied or detected; immutable inputs use a content-hashed read-only allowlist and poison controls prove ambient and prior-run sentinels remain untouched. An observed candidate, helper, or candidate-owned-provider breach is a product failure; lost enforcement evidence or a harness-owned-provider isolation failure is invalid. The manifest content-addresses runner, analyzer, oracle, deterministic suite, evidence-tool limits, seeded product configuration, state and copy schemas, isolation, provider, sampling and median summary, exact platform API and unit schema, complete-workload process topology, and host monitor. One versioned normalization maps only fresh endpoint paths, sockets, ports, provider roots, and endpoint identifiers to role tokens for command and non-secret environment identity; every other command and environment semantic remains exact, and concrete values remain evidence. A single-process topology starts no candidate child at any point; a real helper topology owns every transient and retained member and passes aggregation and membership controls. Host monitoring brackets each pair; ambient faults are invalid while candidate-caused pressure is a product failure. Analyzer boundary, missing-sample, namespace, host, and topology tests pass. Exact source commits, trees, Nix output and NAR, commands, platform and host identities, and evidence are recorded. Local overrides remain command-scoped and absent from committed configuration; N1 either retains the harness explicitly or deletes it.

** Acceptance Criteria

The M2 bundle names a passing M1 and crosswalks the complete ordinary result manifest: protocol revision; immutable product commits and source tree; Nix pins and patch set; derivation, store path, and NAR; runtime, wrapper, gallery, seeded product configuration, budgets, history-dataflow inventory, fixtures and prepared stores, oracle manifest, deterministic suite, evidence-tool limits, deterministic/RSS phase rules, harness and state schema/seed, isolation and provider identities, runtime adapter source and executable hashes, platform API/unit schema, complete-workload topology, host, OS, normalized command template, and normalized non-secret environment manifest; only fresh endpoint paths, sockets, ports, provider roots, and endpoint identifiers may map to declared role tokens, while concrete values remain evidence. If every behavior-affecting identity equals M1, M2 records that proof and M1 remains the measurement for identical bits and environment. If any identity differs, M2 reruns the required inventory review, full four-scale deterministic conformance and online staircase, two order-balanced endpoint pairs, cold diagnostics, and harness controls on the activation-eligible package with unchanged rules and 32 MiB threshold. Only pass qualifies; any product, deterministic, identity, or RSS failure blocks activation and publication. Invalid ambient or harness evidence may be rerun after correction and remains retained. An invalid RSS replay restarts all four endpoints in a new retained bundle; an invalid non-RSS invocation repeats its complete prescribed invocation in a new retained bundle.

OPEN and blocked by M1 and P1. No M2 identity check or replay has run. M2 is the final activation gate, not a mandatory duplicate measurement when the exact passing product and evidence identities are already equal.

:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0

Programme L1. Give the runtime host explicit repository ownership. Long operations capture session identity, generation/snapshot, and cancellation or a lease. Replacement stops new work, aborts and drains old work, detaches consumers, closes old resources, then publishes and binds the new session.

** Acceptance Criteria

New, resume, fork, import, reload, failed construction, repeated close, quit, delayed UI, export, scan, extension, and RPC barriers either finish against an owned snapshot or return typed stale/closed/abort results; raw closed-database errors fail; repositories, connections, statements, descriptors, leases, and spools return to baseline.

Programme E1. Resolve SessionManager.inMemory, --no-session, and the pre-first-assistant publication window. Use a bounded ephemeral repository from the first record or record a maintainer-approved explicit exclusion with a hard admission boundary; never silently accumulate an unlimited transcript.

** Acceptance Criteria

A large pre-assistant/no-session fixture stays within declared budgets or fails before accumulation at the documented exclusion boundary; publication is atomic and permission-safe; failure leaves recoverable state and no leaked private temporary; the contract and docs name the behavior exactly.

Owner decision 2026-08-10: remove the inert public de --no-cache compatibility token and its documentation. The option has no observable cache behavior, no tracked caller, and retaining it falsely promises cache control. After removal, stale callers must fail visibly rather than silently taking the ordinary cached path. Historical context: commit 0bf3c637 removed the already-unused NOCACHE variable; signed commit 21048e822dcfd68e52a8f750735540c91b8b6433 still contains the compatibility branch.

** Acceptance Criteria

bin/de no longer recognizes --no-cache; bin/README.md no longer advertises it; a focused test proves the retired option does not silently run the normal cached path; ordinary environment creation remains unchanged.

** Notes

Authority resolved by the owner: delete the no-op option.

Owner decision 2026-08-10: git-ai is a genuine shared-work host, but it is currently offline and has not been used recently. Represent it in the typed host authority without treating membership as evidence of present availability or including it implicitly in the active four-host rollout. Then project shell normalization and flake-output routing from that authority, retain explicit active-rollout data, and add parity, FQDN, idempotence, refusal, build, and switch-dispatch tests. Sources: validated VCR-005 and VCR-028; abstraction finding 10.

** Acceptance Criteria

git-ai is present in canonical shared-work membership; active rollout membership remains separately explicit and excludes dormant git-ai until deliberately reactivated; shell routing no longer maintains an independent identity table; tests and documentation distinguish known membership from current rollout availability.

Authority resolved by the owner: supported shared-work identity, presently dormant.

forgetConsumedTurnsForChildWithStorage deletes a child across all parents without acquiring those parents tuple locks. A concurrent profile migration can load a source parent ledger, child cleanup deletes the source row, then migration imports the stale row into the target and resurrects consumed state. Enumerate and lock the exact affected profile/parent tuples in deterministic order, revalidate under lock before delete, and prove cleanup racing migration cannot reintroduce the child while unrelated parents remain concurrent.

* DONE [#B] Prevent executable discovery from traversing protected macOS filesystem roots
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-vb0k
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-13 Thu 16:35]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-13 Thu 22:09]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-13 Thu 21:59]
:CLOSE_REASON: Completed the narrow executable-policy/grant/test remediation; dropped speculative cross-client OS confinement and TCC supervisor expansion.
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

A broad filesystem search for a development tool crossed macOS protected roots. At 14:19:41, unified sandbox logs recorded 23 indirect File Provider domain access checks whose subject was the Nix Python 3.14.6 executable used by Agent Deck processes. This aligns with the broad traversal; the user saw Python-labeled prompts for Photos, Music/Media Library, external volumes, and other protected locations. The retained logs do not expose the originating Python PID or independently preserve each user-visible prompt category. Executable discovery must use the repository Nix development environment, not recursive traversal of home, volumes, cloud domains, or protected library roots.

** Acceptance Criteria

Agent tooling never searches for executables by recursively traversing home, filesystem roots, mounted volumes, Photos, Music/Media Library, or other TCC-protected locations. Missing development tools are added to flake.nix, the environment is regenerated with de, and commands run through direnv exec. A regression test or enforceable guard rejects broad protected-root discovery, and diagnostics identify the initiating command without exposing private paths or payloads.

A migrated parent may have no remaining consumed row in the caller profile by the time child cleanup retries or the process crashes. In-memory parent enumeration is then lost even though a lineage-linked target still contains the child fingerprint. Seed recovery from bounded, validated full-lineage migration receipts tied to the caller profile; never scan or delete unrelated profile ledgers. Delete receipt-linked targets before the caller/source and prove injected cross-DB failure plus process-style retry cannot strand or resurrect the child.

* DONE [#B] Fence all already-open schema-19 StateDB writers after schema-20 cutover
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-x0ox
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-13 Thu 17:56]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-13 Thu 22:00]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-13 Thu 22:00]
:CLOSE_REASON: Dropped with the schema-20 synthetic-scale consumed-turn optimization and its multi-release writer-fencing programme. Retain the simpler prior ledger model.
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

The schema-20 writer-epoch check is runtime-mutation scoped. Ordinary metadata save transactions and existing read-write handles can continue without consulting it, so an already-open schema-19 Agent Deck process may mutate a schema-20 database even after the consumed-turn rollout fence is in place. This is broader than inbox deduplication and must be enforced for every legacy writable process or write transaction.

** Acceptance Criteria

After schema-20 cutover, no already-open schema-19 process can commit any StateDB mutation. Every writable entry point either participates in one shared compatibility fence or the upgrade path proves every legacy writer quiesced before migration. A cross-process regression pauses an old writer before an ordinary non-runtime mutation, performs cutover, and proves the mutation is rejected without partial state. Schema-19 rollback remains unsupported and documented.

** Dependencies

#+begin_src json
[

:FINISHED: [2026-08-16 Sun 13:17]
:CLOSE_REASON: Repaired the packaged Pi Mem cache-validity check so a fresh empty-string summary is reusable while the existing 15-minute invalidation remains intact. Added a three-fresh-process packaged-extension regression that proves empty-cache creation, byte-identical reuse after a qualifying sentinel appears, and stale-cache rebuild. The test-only mutant failed with widgetCalls 1 != 0; the repaired aarch64-darwin Pi Gallery check passed at /nix/store/nyj9dksxdwqnvybhbni9m7v59rj2zz18-pi-gallery-check, and config/ai all-system no-build evaluation passed. All 21 Python suites passed: the canonical aggregate reached its fixed 900-second envelope with zero test failures while update-overlay-slow was running; that suite then passed 102 tests directly and the six unreached suites passed 43 tests directly. Minimality and architecture reviews were clear. Pi Mem remains packaged and projected but excluded from automatic Gallery loading, so no Pi session or runtime activation was performed.
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

Pi-mem synchronously scans 130 legacy session directories during every session_start when the resulting daily summary is empty. Its cache test treats an empty summary as a cache miss, so Sana paid 73.4 seconds in the full-Gallery baseline and about 53 seconds in the Lens-disabled run even though no recent JSONL qualified. Make a valid empty cached summary reusable, retain cache invalidation semantics, add a deterministic empty-result regression, and measure restart readiness without changing other Pi extensions.

* DONE [#B] Reproduce Clio Path Finder SMB mount failure
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-xrm
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-11 Tue 10:15]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-12 Wed 20:51]
:ASSIGNEE: johnw
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-12 Wed 20:51]
:CLOSE_REASON: Fresh owner-operated Clio reproduction at 20:44:19 PDT identified the failure boundary. Path Finder initiated a LaunchServices open; CoreServicesUIAgent sent a GURL event to Jump Desktop, which received it. Finder remains the default smb/cifs handler and Jump declares only jump/vnc/rdp, so there is no persistent SMB handler takeover. No NetAuth, NetFS, SMBClient, mount_smbfs, mount, or helper activity occurred; no local mount-point placeholder was created. The private literal scheme was not read, so a saved recent value is not claimed, but an ordinary SMB request is conclusively ruled out: Path Finder handed off a remote-control-class URL or explicitly targeted Jump before native SMB. Treat Path Finder's handoff/server-type coercion as the defect; use Finder Connect to Server as the safe comparison/workaround, and do not mutate LaunchServices. No preferences, credentials, mounts, files, or host state were changed.
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

Path Finder 26.1.7 is independent of Nix Samba. During the reported attempt it produced no NetAuth, NetFS, mount_smbfs, or SMB negotiation evidence; a nearby private URL was routed to Jump Desktop. DNS, TCP 445, and Vulcan Samba are healthy. Reproduce the exact Path Finder server/share action on Clio without exposing credentials, identify whether URL routing, authentication, or native mounting fails, and explain any local mount-point placeholder behavior.

:CLOSE_REASON: Completed in the frozen Gitea publication transaction: literal/revalidated authority, isolated fresh ref snapshot, exact signed tip and raw-history range, conflicting dry-run/publish rejection, supported transactional recovery only, replacement/graft/shallow defenses, and signal-safe post-push readback state. Production fetch obeys the configured remote.origin.fetch, but signature verification excludes every local refs/remotes/origin/* ref. A narrowed refspec can leave a stale tracking ref that hides an unsigned commit not present on Gitea; tests currently append a broad heads refspec and mask this. Acceptance: fetch into explicit fresh temporary refs from the validated Gitea URL and compute the signed range only from those refs; narrowed/stale refspec regressions fail closed.

* DONE [#B] Restore Pi defaults and diagnose startup latency
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-yojh
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-14 Fri 00:31]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-14 Fri 11:32]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-14 Fri 11:32]
:CLOSE_REASON: Restored the exact always-on Pi extension contract and produced an evidence-backed startup and /new cause map without implementing unapproved behavior changes or restarting sessions.
:SOURCE_REPO: nix
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /Users/johnw/src/nix
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

Pi startup and /new are unacceptably slow with the user-required managed extension set. The requested work is diagnosis: identify why the exact always-on profile is slow. The previous attempted fix instead moved Nix Gallery, Pi Loop, MCP Adapter, and Quiet out of the automatic extension directory. That changed required default behavior without authorization and must be reversed.

The always-on extension set is an invariant: Fleet Theme, Nix Gallery and its complete declared member set, Pi Loop, Pi MCP Adapter, and Quiet remain automatically active on every managed host. Restore that state, then produce an evidence-backed cause map for startup and /new with the exact full profile. Do not disable, demote, require explicit flags for, or otherwise change any declared default extension. Do not implement further latency optimizations without presenting the diagnosis and obtaining user approval.

** Acceptance Criteria

1. Every managed Pi profile automatically activates Fleet Theme, Nix Gallery with its complete declared member set, Pi Loop, Pi MCP Adapter, and Quiet; no explicit -e flag is needed.
2. Tests and documentation assert the restored always-on contract and no longer describe declared extensions as opt-in.
3. Startup and /new are measured or traced with the exact rendered always-on profile, and blocking contributors are ranked with measured evidence separated from static hypotheses.
4. The diagnosis identifies the narrowest candidate fixes and their behavioral tradeoffs, but no latency optimization is implemented without explicit user approval.
5. No Agent Deck or Pi session is restarted by this work; activation remains separately authorized.

** Notes

User correction on 2026-08-14: the declared Pi extensions are always wanted on all systems. The prior opt-in interpretation was unauthorized scope expansion. Restore first; diagnose second; ask before changing behavior.

* DONE [#C] Adopt the final declarative Nix builder pool
:PROPERTIES:

:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

Hera currently has the user-activated intermediate three-row /etc/nix/machines (Vulcan, Andoria-08, Andoria-T2). The final declarative source instead emits Vulcan plus the working sudo-daemon Andoria-08 route and removes T2 until nix-l8r is resolved. Build and review the final generation, then activate only with explicit owner authorization; never edit /etc/nix/machines directly.

** Acceptance Criteria

A separately authorized Hera switch adopts the final generated two-row machines file; /etc/nix/machines remains a nix-darwin symlink to the matching Nix-store source; the daemon reloads and becomes ready; a default source-free aarch64-linux build routes to Vulcan; a fresh local-dependency x86_64-linux build routes to Andoria-08 through the declared daemon handoff; no nix-builder or Andoria-T2 row remains; activation and runtime evidence are recorded without claiming Clio adoption.

* DONE [#C] Stabilize Claude fork argv capture against partial log reads
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nix-zvn0
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-13 Thu 13:59]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-13 Thu 14:06]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-13 Thu 14:06]
:CLOSE_REASON: Claude argv capture waits for the complete expected fork argument set; count=20 normal and count=10 race stress lanes pass.
:SOURCE_REPO: nix

:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

Stress-running the Claude fork-start dispatch regressions revealed that readCapturedClaudeArgv returns as soon as the capture log is merely nonempty. It can read a partially written argv list before the final --fork-session argument lands, producing intermittent false failures independent of lifecycle cleanup.

** Acceptance Criteria

The capture helper waits for a complete expected argv record without fixed sleeps; both fork-start dispatch cases pass at least count=20 normally and count=10 under race after lifecycle teardown uses the maintained Instance API.
